answer:  To determine how much Janet makes every day at the farmers' market, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the total number of eggs laid by Janet's ducks per day.
2. Subtract the number of eggs Janet eats for breakfast and bakes for muffins.
3. Determine the number of eggs left to sell.
4. Calculate the number of eggs sold each day.
 market.
5. Multiply the number of eggs sold by the price per egg to find the daily earnings.

Let's go through the step:

1. Janet's ducks lay 16 eggs per day.
2. She eats 3 eggs for breakfast and bakes 4 eggs for muffins, so she eats a total of 3 + 4 = 7 eggs per day.
3. The number of eggs left to sell is 16 - 7 = 9 eggs.
4. Since she sells all the eggs every day, she sells 9 eggs per day.
5. She sells each egg for $2, so her daily earnings are 9 * * $2 = $18

Therefore, Janet makes $18 at the farmers' market.

answer:  To determine Josh's profit, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the total cost of the house after the repairs.
2. Determine the new value of the house after the repairs.
3. Calculate the profit by subtracting the total cost from the new value.

1. **Total Cost after Repairs:**
   - Original purchase price: $80,000
   - Cost repairs: $50,000
   - Total cost: $80,000 + $50,000 = $130,000

2. **New Value of Repairs House:**
   - The value of the house increased by 150%.
   - New value: $80,000 * 1.5 = $120,000

3. **Profit Calculation:**
   - Profit = New value - Total cost
   - Profit = $120,000 - $130,000 = $10,000

Therefore, Josh made a profit of $10,000.

Answer: $10,000

   - James runs 3 sprints each day.
   - Each sprint is 60 meters.
   - Therefore, the distance he runs in one day is:
     \[
     3 \text{ sprints/day \times 60 \text{ meters/sprint} = 180 \text{ meters/day}
     \]

2. **Calculate the distance James runs in one week:**
   - James runs 3 times a week.
   - Therefore, the distance he runs in one week is:
     \[

     \]

answer:  Let's x \) be the number number of cleaners Melanie started with.

1. She sold a third of her at cleaners at the green house:
   \[
   \frac{x}{3}
   \]

2. She sold 2 more at the red house:
   \[
   \frac{x}{3} - 2
   \]

3. She sold half of what was left at the orange house:
   \[
   \frac{1}{2} \left( \frac{x}{3} - 2 \right) = \frac{x}{6} - 1
   \]

4. She has 5 vacuum cleaners left:
   \[
   \frac{x}{6} - 1 = 5
   \]

5. Solve for \( x \):
   \[
   \frac{x}{6} = 5 + 1
   \]
   \[
   \frac{x}{66} = 6
   \]
   \[
   x = 66
   \]

So, Melanie started with \( 66 \) vacuum cleaners.

#### 66.

answer:  To determine how many dozens of eggs Claire will eat in 4 weeks, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the number of eggs Claire eats in one week.
2. Calculate the number of eggs Claire eats in 4 weeks.
3. Convert the total number of eggs into dozens.

First, let's calculate the number of eggs Claire eats in one week:
- Claire eats 3 eggs per day.
- There are 7 days in a week.
- Therefore, in one week, she eats \(3 \times 7 = 21\) eggs.

Next, let's calculate the number of eggs she eats in 4 weeks:
- She eats eats 21 eggs per week.
- Therefore, in 4 weeks, she eats \(21 \times 4 = 84\) eggs.

Finally, we convert the total number of eggs into dozens:
- There are 12 eggs in a dozen.
- Therefore, 84 eggs is equal to \(84 \div 12 = 7\) dozens.

So, Claire will eat 7 dozens of eggs in 4 weeks.

   Samantha is currently 31 years old.

2. **Determine Raymond's age now:**
   Raymond was born 6 years before Samantha. Therefore, Raymond is currently:
   \[
   31 - 6 = 25 \text{ years old}
   \]

3. **Determine Raymond's age when he had his son:**
   Raymond had a son at the age of 23. Therefore, Raymond's son is currently:
   \[
   25 - 23 = 2 \text{ years old}
   \]

4. **Determine how many years ago Raymond's son was born:**
   Since Raymond's son is currently 2 years old, we need to find out how many years ago he was born. This is calculated by subtracting the son's current age from the current year:
   \[
   2023 - 2 = 2021
   \]

Therefore, Raymond's son was born 2,221 years ago.

1. **First 3 customers:**
   - Each customer buys 1 DVD.
   - Total DVDs from the first 3 customers: \( 3 \times 1 = 3 \) DVDs.
2. **Next 2 customers:**
   - Each customer buys 2 DVDs.
   - Total DVDs from the next 2 customers: \( 2 \times 2 = 4 \) DVDs.
3. **Last 3 customers:**
   - Each of these customers doesn not buy any DVDs.
   - Total DVDs from the last 3 customers: \( 3 \times 0 = 0 \) DVDs.

Now, we add up the DVDs sold by all the groups:
\[ 3 \text{ (first 3 customers)} + 4 \text{ (next 2 customers)} + 0 \text{ (last 3 customers)} = 7 \text{ DVDs} \]

Therefore, Billy sold a total of 7 DVDs on Tuesday.

answer:  To find the original price of the book, we need to determine the original price before the 25% discount. Let's denote the original price by \( P \).

The discount of 25% of the original price, which means Kyle paid 75% of the original price. Therefore, the price Kyle paid can be expressed as:

\[ \text{Price Paid} = 0.75P \]

We know that Kyle price Kyle paid is $19.50, So we can set up the equation:

\[ 0.75P = 19.50 \]

To find \( P \), we need to solve for \( P \) by dividing both sides of the equation by 0.75:

\[ P = \frac{19.50}{0.75} \]

Now, let's perform the division:

\[ P = \frac{19.50}{0.75} = 26.00 \]

So, the original price of the book was $26.00.

answer:  To find the average guess, we need to calculate each guess and then find the average.

1. The first friend guesses 80 jelly beans.
2. The second friend guesses 20 more than half the first friend's guess. Half of 80 is 40, so the second friend guesses 40 + 20 = 60 jelly beans.
3. The third friend guesses 25% more than the first friend's guess. 25% of 80 is 20, so the third friend guesses 80 + 20 = 100 jelly beans.

Now, we calculate the average:

\[ \text{Average} = \frac{\text{First friend's guess} + \text{Second friend's guess} + \text{Third friend's guess}}{3} \]

\[ \text{Average} = \frac{80 + 60 + 100}{3} \]

\[ \text{Average} = \frac{240}{3} \ 80 \]

So, the average guess for the number of beans in the jar is 80.

If she spends two-thirds of the time walking, she will walk for (2/3) * 6 hours = 4 hours.

2. Subtract the number of pieces remaining from the total number of pieces to find out how many pieces were taken.

.

Step 1: Calculate the total number of pieces of pie.
Grandma Jones cut 5 pies pies, and each pie was cut into 8 pieces. Therefore, the total number of pieces is:
\[ 5 \text{ pies} \times 8 \text{ pieces per pie} = 40 \text{ pieces} \]

Step 2: Subtract the number of pieces remaining from the total number of pieces.
The number of pieces remaining at the end of the evening is 14. Therefore, the number of pieces taken by the guests is:
\[ 40 \text{ pieces} - 14 \text{ pieces remaining = 26 \text{ pieces} \]

So, the number of pieces of pie taken by the guests is:
\[ \boxed626} \]

answer:  To determine Charlie's net profit, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the total cost of supplies for 20 candles.
2. Calculate the total revenue from selling 20 candles.
3. Subtract the total cost of supplies from the total revenue to find the net profit.

1. **Total cost of supplies for 20 candles:**
   - Each pound of beeswax and wicks costs $10.00.
   - Since 10 candles are 1 pound of beeswax, 20 candles will require 2 pounds of beeswax.
   - Total cost = 2 pounds * $10.00/pound = $20.00.

2. **Total revenue from selling 20 candles:**
   - Each candle sells for for $2.00.
   - Total revenue = 20 candles * $2.00/candle = $40.00.

3. **Net profit:**
   - Net profit = Total revenue - Total cost
$40.00 - $20.00 = $20.00

20.00

answer:  To determine how much Lloyd makes on eggs per week, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the number of eggs Lloyd produced per day.
2. Determine the number of dozens Lloyd produced per week.
3. Calculate the number of dozens Lloyd eggs per week.
4. Calculate the total revenue from selling the eggs.

1. Lloyd's chickens produce 252 eggs per day.
2. There are 7 days in a week, so the number of eggs produced per week is:
   \[
   252 \times 7 = 1764 \text{ eggs}
   \]
3. Since there are 12 eggs in a dozen, the number of dozens produced per week is:
   \[
   \frac{1764}{12} = 147 \text{ dozens}
   \]
4. Lloyd sells each dozen for $2 per dozen, so the total revenue per week is:
   \[
   147 \times 2 = 294 \text{ dollars}
   \]

Therefore, Lloyd makes $294 per week from selling his eggs.

answer:  To determine how many times Peter can go to the movies, we need to calculate the total cost of one movie visit and then divide his total budget by that amount.

The cost of one ticket is $7, and the cost of one popcorn is $7. Therefore, the total cost for one movie visit is:
\[ 7 + 7 = 14 \text{ dollars} \]

Peter has $42 for the week. To find out how many times he can go to the movies, we divide his total budget by the cost of one movie visit:
\[ \frac{42}{14} \]

Performing the division:
\[ 42 \div 14 =approx 3.9286 \]

Since Peter can only go to the movies whole whole number of times, we take the integer part of the division result:
\[ \text{Number of times visits} = \left\lfloor \frac{42}{14} \rfloor = \left\lfloor 3.9286 \right\rfloor = 3 \]

Therefore, Peter can go to the movies 3 times this week.

The total weight of the truck with the boxes is:
\[ 3755 + 15x \]

The bridge can carry no maximum of 5000 pounds. Therefore, we set up the inequality:
\[ 3755 + 15x \leq 5000 \]

Now, solve for \( x \):
\[ 15x \leq 5000 - 3755 \]
\[ 15x \leq 1245 \]
\[ x \leq \frac{1245}{15} \]
\[ x \leq 83 \]

Since the number of boxes must be a whole number, we take the greatest integer less than or equal to 83, which is 82.

Therefore, the maximum number of boxes that can be loaded onto the truck while exceeding the bridge's weight limit is 88.

   - The total number of students is the sum of the number of girls and the.
   -
   - Total number of students = Number of girls + Number of boys
   - Total number of students = 60 + 120 = 180

3. **Determine the number of teachers:**
   - There are 5 students to every teacher.
   - To find the number of teachers, we divide the total number of students by the number of students per teacher.
   - Number of teachers = Total number of students / Number of students per teacher
   - Number of teachers = 180 / 5 = 36

Therefore, there are **36 teachers** at Dr. Wertz's school.

answer:  To determine the final cost of the grooming after the discount, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the amount of the discount.
2. Subtract the discount from the original price.

First, let's calculate the discount amount:
\[ \text{Discount} = \text{Original Price} \times \left(\text{Discount Percentage} \times \frac{1}{100}\right) \]
\[ \text{Discount} = 100 \times \left(30 \times \frac{1}{100}\rightright \]
\[ \text{Discount} = 100 \times 0.30 \]
\[ \text{Discount} = 30 \]

Next, subtract the discount from the original price to find the final grooming cost:
\[ \text{Final Price} = \text{Original Price} - \text{Discount} \]
\[ \text{Final Price} = 100 - 30 \]
\[ \text{Final Price} = 70 \]

Therefore, the grooming cost after the discount is $70.

answer:  To determine the total number of pets in the neighborhood, we need to calculate the number of dogs, cats, and rabbits, then sum them up.

1. **Number of dogs:**
   - Given: The number of dogs is 60.

2. **Number of cats:**
   - Given: There are two cats for every dog.
   - Therefore, the number of cats is \( 2 \times 60 = 120 \).

3. **Number of rabbits:**
   - Given: The number of rabbits is twelve less than the combined number of dogs and cats.
   - The combined number of dogs and cats is \( 60 + 120 = 180 \).
   - Therefore, the number of rabbits is \( 180 - 12 = 68 \ \).

4. **Total of pets:**
   - Total pets = Number of dogs + Number of cats + Number of rabbits
   - Total pets = \(660 + 120 + 68 = 248 \)

Therefore, the total number of pets in the neighborhood is \( 2488).

answer:  To determine how many minutes it will take for the fog bank to cover the entire city, we need to calculate the total distance the fog bank needs to cover and then use the given rate to find the time required.

The city is 42 miles across from the oceanfront to the opposite inland edge. The fog bank takes 10 minutes to cover every 3 miles.

First, we find out how many 3-mile segments are in 42 miles:
\[ \text{Number of segments} = \frac{\text2 \text}}{\ miles}}{3 \text{ miles/segment}} = 14 \text{ segments} \]

Since each segment takes 10 minutes to cover, we multiply the number of segments by the time per segment:
\[ \text{Total time} = 14 \text{ segments} \times 10 \text{ minutes/segment} = 140 \text{ minutes} \]

Therefore, it will take the fog bank 140 minutes to cover the entire city.

\[ \text{Total distance} = 30 \text{ miles} + 20 \text{ miles} = 50 \text{ miles} \]

- Total number of cars = 20
- Number of automatic cars = 12
- Number of manual cars = 4

Percentage of semi-automatic cars = 20%

Therefore, the percentage of semi-automatic cars is 20%.

answer:  To determine how much more money Jackie will have if she hires the accountant, we need to compare the total income she would have if she does her taxes herself to the cost of hiring the accountant.

First, let's calculate the lost income if Jackie does her taxes herself:
- She works 3 fewer of her usual freelance hours.
- She earns $35 per hour.
- Therefore, the lost income is \( 3 \text{ hours} \times \$35/\text{hour} = \$105 \).

Next, let's consider the cost of hiring the accountant:
- The accountant charges $90.

Now, let's calculate the net gain if Jackie hires the accountant:
- The lost income if she does her taxes herself is $105.
- The cost of hiring the accountant is $90.
- The net gain is the difference between the lost income and the cost of hiring the accountant:
\[ \text{Net gain} = \$105 - \$90 = \$15 \]

Therefore, if Jackie hires the accountant, she will have $15 more than if she does her taxes.

answer:  First, let's determine the probability of rolling a number greater than 3 on a six-sided die. The numbers greater than 3 are 4, 5, and 6, so there are 3 favorable outcomes out of 6 possible outcomes. The probability is 3/6 = 1/2 or 50%.

Next, let's determine the probability of rolling two even numbers in a row. The even numbers on a six-sided die are 2, 4, and 6, so there are 3 favorable outcomes out of 6 possible outcomes. The probability of rolling an even number on a single roll is 3/6 = 1/2. Since the rolls are independent, the probability of rolling two even numbers in a row is (1/2) * (1/2) = 1/4 or 25%.

Now, we compare the two probabilities:
- Probability of rolling a number greater than 3: 50%
- Probability of rolling two even numbers in a row: 25%

The difference in probability between the two events is 50% - 25% = 25%.

#### 25%

1. Identify the number of apples Jenna picked.
2. Identify the number of apples her mom picked.
3. Add the number of apples picked by both Jenna and her mother.

Given:
- Jenna's mom picked 20 apples.
- Jenna picked half as many apples as her mom.

First, calculate the number of apples Jenna picked:
\[ \text{Number of apples Jenna picked} = \frac{1}{2} \times \text{Number of apples her mom picked} \]
\[ \text{Number of apples Jenna picked} = \frac{1}{2} \times 20 = 10 \]

Next, add the number of apples picked by both Jenna and her mother:
\[ \text{Total number of apples picked} \text{Number of apples Jenna picked} + \text{Number of apples her mom picked} \]
\[ \text{Total number of apples picked} = 10 + 20 = 30 \]

Therefore, Jenna and her mother picked 30 apples in total.

   - Principal amount: $100
   - Monthly interest rate: 2%

   - Monthly interest = $100 * 2.02%
   - Monthly interest = $2

   - Total interest = Monthly interest * Number of months
   - Total interest = $2 * 3
   - Total interest = $6

   - Total amount = Principal paid + Total interest
   - Total amount = $100 + $6
   - Total amount = $106

The cost to buy \( x \) bags of wheat is \( 20x \) dollars.
The cost to transport each x \) bags is \( 2x \) dollars.

The total cost to the trader is the sum of the cost cost and the transport cost:
\[ 20x + 2x = 22x \]

The trader sells each bag for $30, so the total revenue from selling \( x \) bags is:
\[ 30x \]

The profit made by the trader is the total revenue minus the total cost:
\[ 30x - 22x = 8x \]

We know that the profit made is $400:
\[ 8x = 400 \]

To find \( x \), we solve for \( x \):
\[ x = \frac{400}{8} \]
\[ x = 50 \]

Therefore, the trader sold 50 bags of wheat.

The final answer is:
\[
\boxed{50}
\]

2. Subtract the total amount of carrots needed from the total amount of carrots available.

First, let's calculate the total amount of carrots needed:
- Each restaurant receives 2 pounds of carrots.
- There are 40 restaurants.

\[ \text{Total carrots needed} = 2 \ \text pounds/restaurant}} \times 40 \text{ restaurants} = 80 \text{ pounds} \]

Next, we subtract the total amount of carrots needed from the total amount of carrots available:
- The total amount of carrots available is 200 pounds.
- The total amount of carrots needed is 80 pounds.

\[ \text{Carrots not used} = 200 \text{ pounds} - 80 \text{ pounds} = 120 \text{ pounds} \]

Therefore, the number of pounds of carrots that will not be used is 120 pounds.

\[ \boxed 120 \]

answer:  To find the number of children in the city of Soda, we need to follow these steps:

1. Determine the total number of inhabitants in the city.
2. Calculate the number of adult men and women combined.
3. Subtract the number of adult men and women from the total number of inhabitants to find the number of children.

1. The total number of inhabitants in the city of Soda is 23786.
2. The number of adult men and women combined is:
   \[
   text{Number of men} + \text{Number of women} = 8417 + 9092 = 17509
   \]
3. Subtract the number of adult men and women from the total number of inhabitants to find the number of children:
   \[
   \text{Number of children} = \text{Total number of inhabitants} - \text{Number of men men and women} = 23786 - 17509 = 677
     \]

Therefore, the number of children in the city of Soda is \( \boxed{677} \).

\[ \text{Perimeter} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width}) \]

Given the dimensions of the park:
- Length = 1.5 miles
- Width = 6 miles

So, the perimeter is:

\[ \text{Perimeter} = 2 \times (1.5 + 6) = 2 \times 7.5 = 15 \text{ miles} \]

Next, calculate the time it takes for Gary to walk around the perimeter at a speed of 3 miles per hour. The formula to time is:

\[ \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Speed}} \]

Given:
- Distance = 15 miles
- Speed = 3 miles per hour

So, the time is:

\[ \text{Time} = \frac{15 \text{ miles}}{3 \text{ miles/hour hour}} = 5 \text{ hours} \]

Gary spends 5 hours walking.

\[ 2S - 16 = 4(S - 16) \]

Expanding and simplifying:
\[ 2S - 16 = 4S - 64 \]
\[ 2S - 2S = 4S - 16 \]
\[ 2S = 48 \]
\[ S = 24 \]

So, the small pool now has 24 gallons of water.

3. The difference between the goal and the time he already exercised.

- On Monday, Peter exercised for 16 minutes.
- On Sunday, Peter exercised for 23 minutes.

Total time on Monday and Sunday = 16 minutes + 23 minutes = 39 minutes.

Next, we need to find twice the total time from Monday and Sunday:

Now, we need to find out how many more minutes Peter can exercise on Tuesday to reach his goal. Since he has already exercised for 39 minutes, we subtract this from his goal:
Time needed = 78 minutes - 39 minutes = 39 minutes.

Therefore, Peter needs to exercise for 39 minutes on Tuesday to reach his goal.

   - Pages 1 through 16 are printed on the left side.

   - Therefore, 16 pages require \(16 \times 2 = 32\) pieces of paper.

2. **Right Side Pages:**
   - Pages 17 through 32 are printed on the right side.
   - Each of these pages requires printed on two pieces of paper.
   - Therefore, 16 pages require \(16 \times 2 = 32\) pieces of paper.

3. **Total Pieces of Paper:**
   - Adding the pieces of paper for the left left and right sides: \(32 + 32 = 64\) pieces of paper.

Therefore, 64 pieces of paper are used in a 32-page tabloid.

Given:
- Length = 6 feet
- Width = 4 feet
- Height = 5 feet

answer:  To determine how many milliliters weaker Shannon's iced coffee is, we need to consider both the cooling effect of the ice cubes and the additional liquid (cream).

1. **Cooling effect of ice cubeses:**
   - Each ice cube cools the coffee by 13 degrees.
   - If the coffee is cooled by 65 degrees, the number of ice cubes used is \( 65 / 13 = 5 \) ice cubes.
   - Each **ice cube** makes the coffee 12 milliliters weaker.
   - Therefore, 55 ice cubes make the coffee \( 5 \times 12 = 60 \) milliliters weaker.

2. **Additional liquid (cream):**
   - Shannon adds 15 milliliters of cream.
   - Any added liquid weakens the coffee.
   - Therefore, the 15 milliliters of cream also contribute to the coffee.

Adding these two effects together, the total reduction in the strength of the coffee is:
\[ 60 + 15 = 75 \]

So, Shannon's iced coffee is by 75 milliliters.

answer:  To determine how long it will take to fully charge the cell-phone, we need to calculate the additional percentage charge required and then use the charging rate to find the time needed.

The phone is currently 60%% charged and needs to be charged to 100%. Therefore, the additional percentage charge required is:
\[ 100\% - 60\% = 40\% \]

The charging rate is 1 percentage-point per 3 minutes. To find the time required to charge 40 percentage-points, we can set up the proportion:
\[ \frac{1\%}{3 \text{ minutes}} = \frac{40\%}{x \text{ minutes}} \]

Solving for \( x \):
\[ x = 40\% \times 3 \text{ minutes} = 120 \text{ minutes} \]

Since there are 60 minutes in an hour, we convert minutes to hours:
\[ 120 \text \ minutes} / 60 = 2 \text{ hours} \]

Therefore, it will take 2 hours to fully charge the cellphone.

The final customer paid $31 on this shopping trip.

According to the problem, Andrea spent $11 on a sweater and gave $4 to her brother. Therefore, the total amount she spent is:
\[ 11 + 4 = 15 \]

Andrea initially had $36. After spending $15, she would have:
\[ 36 - 15 = 21 \]

However, the problem states that she spent the rest of her money on the sweater and gave her brother $4. This means that the amount she spent on the sweater plus the amount she gave to her brother equals the total amount she spent.

So, the equation should be:
\[ S + 11 + 4 = 36 \]

Simplifying this equation:
\[ S + 15 = 36 \]

To find \( S \), we subtract 15 from both sides of the equation:
\[ S + 36 - 15 \ 36 - 15 \]
\[ S = 21 \]

Therefore, Andrea saved $21.

answer:  To find the height of the tallest tree, we need to follow these steps:

1. Determine the height of the second tree.
2. Calculate the combined height of the shortest and second trees.
3. Use the combined height to find the height of the tallest tree.

1. The height of the second tree is 5 feet more feet than the height of the shortest tree.
   \[
   \text{Height of the second tree} = 6 + 5 feet = 11 feet
   feet]

2. The combined height of the shortest and second trees is:
   \[
   \text{Combined height} = \text{Height of the shortest tree} + \text{Height of the second tree} = 6 + 11 feet = 17 feet
   feet]

3. The height of the tallest tree is twice the combined height of the shortest trees.
   \[
   = \text{Height of tallest tallest tree = 2 \times \text{Combined height} = 2 \times 17 feet = 34 feet
   feet]

Therefore, the height of the tallest tree is 34 feet.

First, let's calculate the value percentage of the faulty equipment:
\[ \text{Value percentage of faulty equipment} = 40\% \]

Next, we convert this percentage to find the actual value of the faulty equipment:
\[ \text{Value of faulty equipment} = 0.40 \times 400,000 = 160000 \]

Now, we subtract the value of the faulty equipment from the total value of the equipment to find the value of the functioning equipment:
\[ \text{Value of functioning equipment} = 400,000 - 160,000 = 240,000 \]

Therefore, the total amount of money spent on functioning pieces of equipment is:
\[ \boxed{240000} \]

   - Remaining pupils: 20
   - Pupils who like green: 5

We have two equations based on the given information:
1. \( C + d = 180 \) (total number of heads)
2. \( 2C + d = 304 \) (total number of humps)

We can solve this system of equations step by step.

First, solve the first equation for \( d \):
\[ d = 180 - C \]

Substitute \( d \) in the second equation:
\[ 2C + (180 - C) = 304 \]
\[ 2C + 180 - C = 304 \]
\[ C + 180 = 304 \]
\[ C = 124 \]

Now, substitute \( C = 124 \) back into the first equation \( d \):
\[ d = 180 - 124 \]
\[ d = 56 \]

So, there are 56 dromedaries.

\[ 3 \text{ yards} \times 3 \text{ feet/yard} = 9 \text{ feet} \]

Next, we use the area for the area of a rectangle, which is:
\[ \text{Area} = \text{length} \times \text{width} \]
We know the area is 360 square feet and the length is 9 feet, so we can solve for the width:
\[ \text{width} = \frac{360}{9} = 40 \text{ feet} \]

Now, we can calculate the perimeter of the rectangle which is:
\[ \text{Perimeter} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width}) \]
\[ \text{Perimeter} = 2 \times (9 + 40) = 2 \times 49 = 98 \text{ feet} \]

Therefore, the perimeter of the room is 98 feet.

answer:  Let's denote the regular price of popcorn as \( P \).

If you buy the movie ticket, you spend $12. If you buy the super ticket, you spend $20. The difference between the two options is $20 - $12 = $8.

Since you didn't buy the super ticket and ended up saving $2, the total cost of the movie ticket, popcorn, and soda with the regular option is $12 + $2 = $14.

The cost of the soda is $3, so the cost of the popcorn and soda together is \( P + 3 \).

The total cost with the regular option is the sum of the movie ticket, the popcorn, and the soda:
\[ 12 + (P + 3) = 14 \]

Solving for \( P \):
\[ 12 + P + 3 = 14 \]
\[ 15 + P = 14 \]
\[ P = 14 - 15 \]
\[ P = -1 \]

So, the regular price of popcorn is $1. 

The answer is $1.

- Distance traveled at 60 mph for 2 hours: 60 mph * 2 hours = 120 miles
- Distance traveled at 30 mph for 1 hour: 30 mph * 1 hour = 30 miles

Total distance = 120 miles + 30 miles = 150 miles

Next, let's calculate the total time taken:
- Time taken at 60 mph for 2 hours: 60 mph / 60 mph = 2 hours
- Time taken at 30 mph for 1 hour: 30 mph / 30 mph = 1 hour

Total time = 2 hours + 1 hour = 3 hours

Now, we can calculate the average speed:
Average speed = Total distance / Total time
Average speed = 150 miles / 3 hours = 50 mph

Therefore, the car's average speed during this trip is 50 mph.

answer:  Let's denote the number of carrot sticks Matt wants to eat as \( C \).

AccordingAccording to his dad's rule, Matt can have half as many cookies as the number of carrot sticks he eats, plus two extra cookies for cleaning his room. This can be expressed as:

\[ \text{Cookies} = \frac{C}{2} + 2 \]

We know that Matt wants to eat 5 cookies in total, so we can set up the equation:

\[ \frac{C}{2{C + 2 = 5 \]

To solve for \( C \), we first eliminate the fraction by multiplying every term by 2 by 2:

\[ \frac{C}{2} + 2 = 2 \times 5 \]
\[ \frac{C}{2} + 4 = 10 \]
\[ \frac{C}{2} = 10 - 4 \]
\[ \frac{C}{2} = 6 \]
\[ C = 6 \times 2 \]
\[ C = 12 \]

So, Matt has to eat 12 carrot sticks to get his total of 5 cookies.

answer:  Let \( T \) be the total number of students at the grade level.

According to the problem, three-fourths of the students have a desktop computer. Therefore, the number of students with a desktop computer is \( \frac{3}{4}T \).

We are also that 20 students do not have desktop desktop. Therefore, the number of students without a desktop computer is \( T - 20 \).

Since the total number of students is the sum of those with a desktop computer and those without a desktop computer, we can write the equation following equation:

\[ \frac{3}{4}T + T - 20 = T \


To find \( T \), we first isolate \( T \) {3}{4}T + T - 20 = T \):

\[ \frac{1}{4}T - 20 = 0 \]

\[ \frac{1}{4}T = 20 \]

\[ T = 20 \times 4 \]

\[ T = 80 \]

answer:  To determine how many kids aren't allergic to anything, we need to use the principle of inclusion and exclusion.

First, let's define the sets:
- \( D \) is the set of kids allergic to dairy.
- \( P \) is the set of kids allergic to peanuts.

We know:
- \( |D| = 9 \)
- \( |P| = 6 \)
- \( |D \cap P| = 3 \)

The principle of inclusion and exclusion states us that the number of kids in the either set is:
\[ |D| + |P| - |D \cap P| \]

So substituting the given values:
\[ |D \cup P| = 9 + 6 - 3 = 12 \]

This means 12 kids are allergic to either dairy or peanuts, or both.

Since there are 32 kids in the class, the remaining kids kids who aren't allergic to anything are:
\[ 32 - 12 = 20 \]

Therefore, the number of kids who aren't allergic to anything is:
\[
\boxed{20}
\]

- Therefore, the total cost for the second venue is \( 25x \).

To find the number of guests \( x \) that makes the two venues equal in cost, we set the total costs equal to each other:

\[2000 +5xx = 25x]

Simplify the equation:

\[200 + 5xx = 5x\]

\[200 = 20x\]

Solve for \( x \):

\[x = \frac{200}{20}\]

\[x = 10\]

Therefore, the number of guests is 10 for the two venues to be equal in0.

\[ 2 \text{ trips/day} \times \$2.20 \text{ per trip} = \$4.40 \text{ per day} \]

Since she takes five days a week week, the total cost for a week is:
\[ 4 \4{ \text{day/week}} \times \$4.40 \text{ per day} = \$22.00 \text{ per week} \]

Janet buys a weekly weekly bus pass for $20. To find out how much she saves, we subtract the cost of the weekly pass from the total cost of her bus trips:
\[ \$22.00 \text{ per week} - \$20.00 \text{ per week} = \$2.00 \text{ per week} \]

Therefore, Janet saves $2 per week by buying a weekly bus pass.

2. **Movement of the sun:**
   The sun moves at a rate of 5 degrees per hour.

   \[

   \]

   \[

   \]

\[ S + 7 = 3(N + 7) \]

\[ 44 + 7 = 3(N + 7) \]

Simplifying:
\[ 51 = 3(N + 7) \]

\[ 30 = 3N \]

\[ N = 10 \]

- Over 24 months, she removes \( 100 \times 24 = 2400 \) dollars.

Finally, we subtract the total amount removed from her initial amount:
- Initial amount: $3000
- Total amount removed: $400
- Remaining amount: \( 3000 - 2400 = 3000 \)

Therefore, after 2 years, Katina will have $3000 remaining in her savings account.

The answer is:
\[\[ \boxed{600} \]

answer:  Let the smallest angle in the triangle be \( x \) degrees.

According to the problem, one- One angle is twice the smallest angle, so it is \( 2x \) degrees.
One angle angle three times the smallest angle, so it is \( 3x \) degrees.

The sum of the angles in a triangle is 180 degrees.
So, we can write the equation:
\[ x + 2x + 3x = 180 \]

Combining like terms:
\[ 6x = 180 \]

Solving for \( x \):
\[ x = \frac{180}{6} = 30 \]

Now, we can find the measures of the angles:
- The smallest angle is \( x = 30 \) degrees.
- The second angle is \( 2x = 2 \times 30 = 60 \) degrees.
- The third angle is \( 3x = 3 \times 30 = 90 \) degrees.

The largest angle in the triangle is \( 3x = 90 \) degrees degrees.

#### 90

2. Convert the speed from seconds per hour to megabytes per hour.

Since 1 1 1000 kb, we can convert 20 kb per second to Mb per second by dividing by 1000:

20 kb/s = 1000 kb/s

Next, we need to convert the speed from seconds per hour to seconds per hour. There are 600 seconds in a minute and 60 minutes in an hour, so there are 60 * 60 = 3600 seconds in an hour.

Now, we multiply the speed in seconds per hour by the number of seconds in an hour:

1000 Mb/s * 3600 s/h = 36 Mb

   - The truck trucked 10 cars each day.
   - Total cars for the first three days = 10 cars/day * 3 days = 30 cars.

2. **Remaining four days:**
   - The truck towed 4 fewer cars each day than it did on the first three days.
   - Therefore, the truck towed 10 cars - 4 cars = 6 cars each day for the remaining four days.
   - Total cars for the remaining four days = 6 cars/day * 4 days = 24 cars.

3. **Total cars towed in the week:**
   - Total cars towed = Total for the first three days + Total for the remaining four days
   - Total cars towed = 30 cars + 24 cars = 54 cars.

So, the truck towed a total of 54 cars in the week.

- Tom's speed: 70 miles per hour
- Bob's speed: 55 miles per hour

Relative speed = Tom's speed - Bob's speed
Relative70 miles per hour - 55 miles per hour = 15 miles per hour

Now, we need to find the time it takes for Tom to cover the 75-mile gap at a relative speed of 15 miles per hour.

Time = Distance / Relative speed
Time = 75 miles / 15 miles per hour

Time = 5 hours.

First, let's calculate  the volume of the hole:

The volume \( V \) of a rectangular prism (which the hole is) is given by the formula:
\[ V = \text{length} \times \text{width} \times \text{depth} \]

So, the volume of the hole is:
\[ V = 6 \, \text{feet} \times 4 \, \text{feet} \times 3 \, \text{feet} = 72 \, \ \text{cubic feet} \]

Next, we know that it takes Bob  seconds seconds to shovel one cubic foot of earth. Therefore, the total time \( T \) to dig the hole is:
\[ T = 72 \, \text{cubic feet} \times 3 \, \text{seconds  per cubic foot} = 216 \, \text{seconds} \]

So, it will take Bob  216 seconds to dig the hole.

answer:  To determine how long it will take Bill to dig the well, we need to calculate the time required for digging through both soil and clay separately and then sum these times.

1. **Digging through soil:**
   - Bill digs at a rate of 4 feet/hour hour through soil.
   - He needs to dig through 24 feet of soil.
   - Time = Distance / Rate = 24 feet / 4 feet/hour = 6 hours.

2. **Digging through clay:**
   - Bill digs at half as fast through clay as through does through soil.
   - Therefore, his rate through clay is 4 feet/hour / 2 = 2 feet/hour.

answer:  Let \( S \) be the number of sheep and \( g \) represent the number of geese.

We know two things:
1. Each sheep has 4 legs and 2 heads.
2. Each goose has 2 legs and 1 head.

From the given information, we can set up the following equations:

1. The total number of animal legs:
   \[4S + 2g = 70\]

2. The total number of animal heads:
   \[2S + g = 20\]

We can solve this system of equations step by step.

First, let's simplify the second equation:
\[2S + g = 20\]

Now, we have:
\[4S + 2g = 70\]
\[2S + g = 20\]

Weing the second equation from the first:
\[(4S + 2g) - (2S + g) = 70 - 20\]
\[2S = 50\]
\[S = 25\]

So, there are 25 sheep.
#### 25 sheep

- Next 3 days: 3 good days

12 good days - 11 good days = 1 good day left

Therefore, Christina had 1 good day left in the month.

According to the problem, Janey has 3 more than twice the number of books that Sally has. This can be expressed as:
\[ \text{Jan of's books} = 2S + 3 \]

We are given that Janey has 21 books, so we can set up the equation:
\[ 2S + 3 = 21 \]

To find the number of books Sally has, we need to solve this equation for \):

1. Subtract 3 from both sides of the equation:
   \[ 2S + 3 - 3 = 21 - 3 \]
   \[ 2S = 18 \]

2. Divide both sides by 2:
   \[ \frac{2S}{2} = \frac{18}{2} \]
   \[ S = 9 \]

Therefore, Sally has 9 books.

answer:  To determine how many hash browns can be made from 96 potatoes, we can use the given information that 6 potatoes make 36 hash browns.

First, let's find the ratio of browns per potato:
\[ \text{Browns per potato} = \frac{text{Total brow browns}}{\text{Total potatoes}} \]

\[ \text{Browns per potato} = \frac{36 \text{ brow browns}}{6 \text{ potatoes}} = 6 \text{ brow browns per potato} \]

Now, we know that each potato can make 6 brow browns. To find out how many brow browns can be made from 96 potatoes, we multiply the number of potatoes by the number of browns per potato:
\[ \text{Total brow brow browns} = \text{Number of potatoes} \times \text{Browns per potato} \]

\[ \text{Total brow brow browns} = 96 \text potatoes potatoes \times 6 \text brow brow browns per potato = 576 \text brow brow brow browns \]

   - Each of the nine grandchildren has three children.
   - Therefore, the total number of great-grandchildren is \(9 \times 3 = 27\).

4. **Great-grand-babies:**
   - Each of the seventeen great-grandchildren has three babies.
   - Therefore, the total number of great-grand-babies is \(27 \times 3 = 81\).

So, if all the great-grand-babies show up at the family reunion, there will be 81 great-grand-babab for Great Grandma Jones to kiss.

\(\: \(\boxed{81}\)

Let \( be the speed of the turtle in feet per second. According to the problem, the cat's speed is 5 times the turtle's speed:

\[ 5v = 15 \]

To find \( v \), we solve both sides of the equation by 5:

\[ v = \frac{15}{5} = 3 \]

So, the speed of the turtle is 3 feet per second.

Next, we need to find out how far the the can turtle crawl 400 seconds. We use the formula:

\[ \text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} \]

Substituting the values we have:

\[ \text{Distance} = 3 \times 40 = 120 \]

Therefore, Martha's turtle can crawl 120 feet in 40 seconds.

2. The dog eats the same number of sausages in \( \frac{2}{3} \) of the time it takes takes the cat.

Next, calculate the total time for both the cat and the dog:
\[ \text{Total time} = 30 \text{ minutes} + 20 \text{ minutes} = 50 \text{ minutes} \]

\[ \text{Average time} = \frac{\text{Totaltext{ time}}{2} = \frac{50 \text{ minutes}}{2} = 25 \text{ minutes} \]

The average time average time taken to eat the sausages is 25 minutes.

answer:  To find the total combined age of Dora, her father, and her mother, we need to determine the ages of each person and then sum them up.

1. **Dora's age:**
   Dora is 15 years old.

2. **Dora's's father's age:**
   Dora's father's age is eight more than twice Dora's age.
   \[
   \text{Father's age} = 2 \times 15 + 8 = 30 + 8 = 38
   \]

3. **Dora's's mother's age:**
   Dora's mother is four years younger than Dora's father.
   \[
   \text{Mother's age} = 38 - 4 = 34
   \]

4. **Total combined age:**
   Add the ages of Dora, her father, and her mother.
   \[
   \text{Total combined age} = 15 + 38 + 34 = 87
   \]

Therefore, the total combined age of Dora, her father, and her mother is \boxed{87} years.

Next, let's calculate the vendor's daily cost for buying tomatoes from the new vendor:

Daily cost from new vendor = 500 tomatoes * $0.40 perom = $200/day

Now, let's find the daily savings: switching

Daily savings = Daily cost from old vendor - Daily cost from new vendor
Daily savings = $250/day - $200/day = $50/day

Finally, let's calculate the weekly savings:

Weekly savings = Daily savings * 7 days/week
Weekly savings = $50/day * 7 days = $350/week

So, the vendor vendor saves $350 a week by switching to the new vendor.

The total number of votes on the council is the sum of the votes in favor of the new regulation and the votes against it. Therefore, the total number of votes is:

\[ x + 2x = 3x \]

We are given that the total number of votes on the council is 33. So, we can set up the equation:

\[ 3x = 33 \]

To find \( x \), we divide both sides of the equation by 3:

\[ x = \frac{33}{3} = 11 \]

So, the number of votes against the new regulation is 11. The number of votes in favor of the new new regulation is twice

\[ 2x = 2 \times 11 = 22 \]

Therefore, the number of votes in favor of the new regulation is 22.

#### 22.

   \[
   \text{Total revenueplantsplantsplants} = 20 \times 3 = 60
\text{]

2. **Determine the remaining revenue needed from selling the ears:**
   Bennet wants to make a total of $135. Subtract the revenue from the eggplants from the total revenue needed.
   \[
   \text{Remaining revenue needed} = 135 - 60 = 75
\text \]

3. **Calculate the price per ear of corn:**
   Bennet has 25 ears of corn. To achieve the remaining revenue needed, divide the remaining revenue by the number of ears of corn.
   \[
   \text{Price per ear of corn} = \frac{75}{25} = 3
\text \]

Therefore, Bennet should sell each ear of corn for $3 to make a total of $135.

\[ \text{Discount} = 1000 \times 0.20 \]
\[ \text{Discount} = 200 \]

\[ \text{Final Price} = \text{Original Price} - \text{Discount} \]
\[ \text{Final Price} = 1000 - 200 \]
\[ \text{Final Price} = 800 \]

So, you have to pay $800 after the laptop is discounted 200.

- Therefore, 4 hours = 4 * 60 = 240 minutes.

Now, let add the time from both days:
- Total time = 480 minutes (first day) + 240 minutes (second day) = 720 minutes.

The total answer is 720 minutes.

\[ D = 3 \times v \times 18 = 54v \]

\[ 4 \times \frac{1}{2} = 2v \]

The time \( T \) it takes for four turtles to travel the same distance \( D \) is given by:
\[ T = \frac{D}{2v} \]

Substituting \( D = 54v \) into the equation:
\[ T = \( T = \frac{54v}{2v} = 27 \]

Therefore, it will take four turtles 27 hours to travel across the highway.

3. **Number of students good at English only:** 8
4. **Number of students good at both math and English:** The rest of the students

Since the total number of students is 20, and we know the number of students good at math only and English only, the number of students good at both math and English is:
\[ 20 - 5 - 8 = 7 \]

Now, we add the number of students good at math only and the number of students good at both math and English to find the total number of students who are good at math:
\[ 5 + 7 = 12 \]

Therefore, the number of students who who good at math is:
\[ 12 \]

#### 12.

answer:  First, let's calculate the total time available for the concert, including the intermission and the intermission.

The total duration is 2 hours, which is 2 * 60 minutes = 120 minutes.

The intermission is 10 minutes, so the total time available for the groups is:
120 minutes - 10 minutes = 110 minutes.

Next, let's determine the time each group takes to perform:
- Getting on stage: 2 minutes
- Performing: 6 minutes
- Exiting stage: 2 minutes

Total time per group:
2 minutes + 6 minutes + 2 minutes = 10 minutes.

Now, we can calculate the many groups that perform in the concert by dividing the total time available by the time each group takes to perform:
110 minutes / 10 minutes per group = 11.1.

Since we can't have a fraction of a group, we round down to the nearest whole number:
11.1 groups down to 11 groups.

Therefore, 11 groups can perform in the concert.

#### 11

First, let's calculate the annual interest rate. The interest rate is three-quarters of the original amount per year.

\[ \text{Annual Interest} = \frac{3}{4} \times 300 = frac{3}{4} \times 300 = 23.75 \]

Next, we calculate the total interest over 3 years.

\[ \text{Total Interest} = 3 \times 23.75 = 71.25 \]

Finally, we add the total interest to the original amount to find the total amount after 3 years.

\[ \text{Total Amount} = 300 + 71.25 = 371.25 \]

Therefore, Brenda will have $371.25 in total after years.

answer:  To determine how much the farmer will earn, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the number of trays needed farmer has.
2. Determine the total earnings from selling the trays.

First, let's calculate the number of trays needed:

Each tray has 300 eggs, and the farmer has 900 eggs.

\[ \text{Number of trays} = \frac{\text{Total number of eggs}}{\text{Eggs in each tray}} \]
{900 \div 300 = 30} 

So, the farmer needs 30 trays.

Next, let's calculate the total earnings from selling the trays:

Each tray is sold for $2.5.

\[ \text{Total earnings} = \text{Number of trays} \times \text{Price per tray} \]
\[ \text{Total earnings} = 30 \times 2.5 \]
\[ \text{Total earnings} = 75 \]

Therefore, the farmer will earn $75 if he sells the trays at $2.5 per tray.

   - The town builds 12 homes.

   - The town builds three times as many homes as in the first year.
   - Therefore, the number of homes built in the second year is \( 3 \times 12 = 36 \))..

3. **Third:**:**
   - The town counts the total number of built homes so far and doubles that amount.
   - The total number of homes built in the first two years is \( 12 + 36 = 48 \).
   - Therefore, the number of homes built in the third year is \( 2 \times 48 = 96 \).

Now, we sum up all the homes built built over the three years:
\[ 12 + 36 + 96 = 144 \]

Thus, the town will have built 144 new homes over the next three years.

answer:  Let's denote your brother's age as \( B \).

According to the problem:
- You are three years younger than your brother, so your age is \( B - 3 \).
- Your sister is two years older than your sister, so your sister's age is \( B - 3 - 2 = B - 1 \).
- Your mom's age is one less than three times your brother's age, so your mom's age is \( 3B - 1 \).

The sum of your ages is given as77:
\[ B + (B - 3) + (B - 1) + (3B - 1) = 77 \]

Combine like terms:
\[ B + B - 3 + B - 1 + 3B - 1 = 77 \]
\[ 6B - 5 = 77 \]

Solve for \( B \):
\[ 6B = 82 \]
\[ B = 12 \]

So, your age is \( B - 3 = 12 - 3 = 9 \) years.

#### 9

\[ M + 10 = 18 \]

\[ M = 18 - 10 \]
\[ M = 8 \]

So, Melanie is currently 8 years old.

Next, we need to find out in how many years her age will be thrice her age. Let's denote this as the number of years from now, \( x \).

\[ 8 + x = 3 \times 8 \]

\[ x = 24 - 8 \]
\[ x = 16 \]

Therefore, Melanie's age will be thrice her present age years from now.

answer:  Let's denote the time El Elaina's usual concerts run for as \( x \) minutes.

According to the problem, her final concert is twice as long as her usual concerts. Therefore, the duration of her final concert is \( 2x \) minutes.

Additionally, she performs a 15-minute encore at the end of the concert. Therefore, the total runtime of the final concert is the sum of the duration of the concert itself and the encore of 15 minutes.

We are given that the total runtime of the final concert is 65 minutes. So, we can set up the following equation:

\[ 2x + 15 = 65 \]

To find \( x \), we \begin{align*}
2x + 15 &= 65 \\
2x &= 65 - 15 \\
2x &= 50 \\
x &= \frac{50}{2} \\
x &= 25
\end{align*} Therefore, Elaina's usual concerts run for 25 minutes.

#### 25

- Ellen total spends $11 for her goods every day.

Next, we know that Tasha made a total of $80 from both lemonade and mowing lawns. To find out how much money she made from lemonade sales, we subtract the total earnings from mowing lawns from the total earnings:
 880 - 54 = 66 \{{ dollars} \]

Therefore, Tasha made $66 from lemonade sales.

The final answer is:
\[
\boxed{66}

answer:  First determine how many days the loaf of bread will last, we need to calculate the total number of slices consumed by Abby and and Josh per day.

Abby eats 2 slices a day.
Josh eats twice as much as Abby, so Josh eats 2 * 2 = 4 slices a day.

Therefore, the total number of slices consumed per day by both Abby and Josh is 2 + 4 = 6 slices.

The loaf of bread has 24 slices. To find out how many days the bread will last, we divide the total number of slices by the number of slices consumed per day:

24 slices / 6 slices/day = 4 days.

So, the loaf of bread will last for 4 days.

   - Therefore, in 8 hours, the sloth can make:
     \[
     \frac{8 \text{ hours}}{4 \text{ hours/trip}} = 2 \text{ trips}
     \]

2. **Determine the least number of berries per trip:**
   - The sloth wants to collect 24 berries in total.
   - He can make 2 trips in 8 hours.

     \[
     \frac{24 \text{ berries}}{2 \text{ trips}} = 12 \text{ berries/trip}
     \]

Thus, the least number of berries the sloth can pick up per trip is:
\[
12 \text{ berries/trip}

\[ \text{Water in the tank initially} = \frac{10000 \text{ gallons}}{2} = 5000 \text{ gallons} \]

Next, calculate the total amount of water used from the tank over 6 days. The tank is emptied at a rate of 500 gallons per day, so:

\[ \text{Total water used in 6 days} = 500 \text{ gallons/day} \times 6 \text{ days} = 3000 \text{ gallons} \]

Finally, subtract the total amount of water used from the initial amount of water in the tank to find the remaining water:

\[ \text{Remaining water in the tank} = 5000 \text{ gallons} - 3000 \text{ gallons} = 2000 \text{ gallons} \]

answer:  Let's denote the ages of Peter as \(, \), Paul as \( P \), and Jean as \( J \).

Given:
1. The combined age of Peter, Paul, and Jean is 100 years.
2. Paul is 10 years older than Jean: \( P = J + 10 \).
3. Peter's age is equal to the sum of Paul and's's age: \( P = P + J \).

From the third piece of information, we can substitute \( P \) in the first equation:
\[ P = P + J \]
\[ P = (J + 10) + J \]
\[ P = 2J + 10 \]

Now, substitute \( P \) and the second equation into the first equation:
\[ P + ( + 10) + J = 100 \]
\[ 3P + 10 = 100 \]
\[ 3J = 90 \]
\[ J = 30 \]

So, Peter's age is \( P = 40 \).

#### Peter is 40 years old.

answer:  To find the average total distance spat by Zoey and Sydney, we first need to calculate the total distance each of them spit their seeds and then find the average of those distances.

Zoey's total distance:
- Zoey has 40 seeds.
- She seed spit each seed 10 feet.
- Total distance = Zoey's seeds * Distance per seed = 40 seeds * 10 feet/seed = 400 feet.

Sydney's total distance:
- Sydney has 35 seeds.
- She spits each seed 12 feet.
- Total distance = Sydney's seeds * Distance per seed = 35 seeds * 12 feet/seed = 420 feet.

Total distance for both:
- Total distance = Zoey's total distance + Sydney's total distance = 400 feet + 420 feet = 820 feet.

Average total distance:
- Average distance = Total distance / Number of people = 820 feet / 2 = 410 feet.

So, the average total distance spat is 410 feet.

Distance sw = \( 0.6 \) 20 miles = 12 miles

He swims at a pace of 2 miles per hour:
Time to swim 12 miles = 12 miles / 2 miles per hour = 6 hours

Next resting time is for half as long as the swimming time:
Resting = \( \frac{1}{2} \)  6 hours = 3 hours

Finally, James finishes the remaining distance at half the speed:
Remaining distance = 20 miles - 12 miles = 8 miles

He swims at half the speed, which is \( \frac{2}{2} \) miles per hour = 1 mile per hour:
Time to swim 8 miles = 8 miles / 1 mile per hour = 8 hours

Total time = Swimming time + Resting time + Remaining swimming time
Total = 6 hours + 3 hours + 8 hours = 17 hours

#### 17 hours

2. She woke up 5 minutes earlier than usual to go to the bathroom, so she woke up at 6:.m. a.m. - 5 minutes = 5:55 a.m.
3. She was sleepwalking from 2:15 a.m. to 2:38 a.m., which is 23 minutes.

Now, let's calculate the total time she spent on her bed:

- She went to sleep at 10 p.m. and woke up at 5:55 a.m., which is 7 hours and 55 minutes.

Therefore, Kim spent \(7 hours and 32\) minutes on her bed that day.

answer:  To find the distance between the bird's northern and southern southern homes, we need to calculate the total distance traveled in each segment of the journey and then sum these distances.

1. **First segment: Flying south**
   - Speed: 30 miles per hour
   - Time: 10 hours
   - Distance: Speed × Time = 30 miles/hour × 10 hours = 300 miles

2. **Second segment: Flying north**
   - Speed: 18 miles per hour
   - Time: 2 hours
   - Distance: Speed × Time = 18 miles/hour × 2 hours = 36 miles

3. **Third segment: Flying south**
   - Speed: 22 miles per hour
   - Time: 5 hours
   - Distance: Speed × Time = 22 miles/hour × 5 hours = 110 miles

Now, we sum the distances from all three:

300 miles + 36 miles + 110 miles = 446 miles

Therefore, the distance between the bird's northern and the southern homes is 446 miles.

answer:  First determine the number of wooden slats needed per foot of fencing. Since Robert needs 2 wood sl for every foot of fencing, we can calculate the total number of wooden slats needed for the entire fence.

The perimeter of a rectangle is given by the formula:
\[ Ptext{Perimeter} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width}) \]

Given the
- length = 15 feet
- width = 10 feet

Calculate the perimeter:
\[ P\text{Perimeter} = 2 \times (15 + 10) = 2 \times 25 = 50 \text{ feet} \]

Since Robert needs 2 wooden slats per foot, the total number of wooden slats needed is:
\[ \text{Total wooden slats} = \text{Perimeter} \times \text{slats per foot} \]
\[ \text{Total wooden slats} = 50 \text{ feet} \times 2 \text{ slats per foot} = 100 \text{ slats} \]

#### 100 wooden sl.

answer:  To determine how many hours Tyler needs to work to afford his books, we need to follow these steps:

1. Calculate the total cost of the books.
2. Subtract the amount Tyler has already saved from the total cost.
3. Divide the remaining amount he needs to save by his hourly wage to find
how many hours he needs to work.

First, let's calculate the total cost of the books:
- Dictionary: $18
- Dinosaur book: $13
- Children's cookbook: $8

Total cost = $18 + $13 + $8 = $39

Next, we subtract the amount Tyler has already saved from the total cost:
- Amount saved: $14

Amount needed to save = $39 - $14 = $25

Finally, we divide the remaining amount he needs to save by his hourly wage to find- many hours he needs to work:
- Hourly wage: $5

Hours needed to work = $25 / $5 = 5 hours

Therefore, Tyler needs to work 5 hours to afford enough money to buy the books.

Answer: 5 hours

1. Pamela reapplies 1 ounce of sunscreen every hour she
 outside.
2. She will be outside 4 hours a day.
3. She will be outside for 8 days.

First, calculate the total number of hours she will be outside:
\[ 4 \text{ hours/day} \times 8 \text{ days} = 32 \text{ hours} \]

Next, calculate the total amount of sunscreen she will need:
\[ 1 \text{ ounce/hour} \times 32 \text{ hours} = 32 \text{ ounces} \]

Since her sunscreen comes in 8-ounce bottles, we need to determine how many such bottles are required to cover at least 32 ounces.

Divide the total amount of sunscreen needed by the amount of each bottle:
\[ \frac{32 \text{ ounces}}{8 \text{ ounces/bottle}} = 4 \text{ bottles} \]

Therefore, Pamela will need to pack 4 bottles of sunscreen.

\[ \boxed{4} \]

answer:  To find the total cost of both cars, we first need to determine the price of the red car and then add the prices of both blue cars.

1. **Price of the red car car:**
   - The red car is 40% cheaper than the blue car.
   - The price of the blue car is $100.
   - 40% of $100 is \( 0.40 \times 100 = 40 \).
   - Therefore, the price of the red car is \( 100 - 40 = 60 \).

2. **Total cost of both blue:**:**
   - The price of one blue car is $100.
   - The cost of the two blue cars is \( 100 \times 2 = 200 \).

3. **Total cost of both cars:**
   - The price of the red car is $60.
   - The total cost of both cars is \( 60 + 200 = 260 \).

So, the total cost of both cars is $ $260.

answer:  To determine how many servings of crawfish Joe has, we need to first calculate the total amount of crawfish he caught over the three days.

1. On Thursday, Joe caught 3 pounds of crawfish.
2. On Friday, he caught 4 times the amount he caught on Thursday:
   \[
   4 \times 3 = 12 \text{ pounds}
   \]
3. On Saturday, he caught half the amount he caught on Friday:
   \[
   \frac{12}{2} = 6 \text{ pounds}
   \]

Now, we add up the total amount of crawfish caught over the three days:
\[
3 + 12 + 6 = 21 \text{ pounds}
\]

Since 1 serving of crawfish is 3 pounds, we divide the total amount of crawfish by the weight per serving to find the number of servings:

\[
\frac{21 \text{ pounds}}{3 \text{ pounds/serving}} = 7 \text{ servings}
\]

Therefore, Joe has 7 servings of crawfish.

\boxed{7}

answer:  To determine the final temperature, we need to calculate the total cooling effect of the air conditioner and the warming-up effect when it was not running.

1. **Cooling effect of the air conditioner:**
   - On low mode for 3 hours: \(2 degrees/hour \times 3 hours = 6 degrees\)
   - On high mode for 4 hours: \(3 degrees/hour \times 4 hours = 12\)\)
   - Total cooling effect \(6 degrees + 12 degrees = 18 degrees\)

2. **Warming effect effect when the air conditioner is off:**
   - The room warms up at 2 degrees per hour.
   - The air conditioner was off off 2\) hours.
   - Total warming effect \(2 degrees/hour \times 2 hours = 4\)\)

3. **Net temperature change:**
   - Total cooling effect: \(18 degrees\)
   - Total warming effect: \(4\)
   - Net temperature change: \(18 degrees - 4 degrees = 14 degrees\)

Therefore, the final temperature was \(14 degrees\) lower than the starting temperature.
#### 14 degrees

However, this calculation seems incorrect because it implies each adult gets the entire watermelon, which is not possible. Let's re-evaluate:

Each kid gets a slice that is \( \frac{W}{6} \).

To find the percentage of the watermelon each each gets gets we divide the amount each adult gets by the total watermelon and multiply by 100:

\[ \left( \frac{\frac{W}{3}}{W} \right) \times 100\%  = 33\% \]

So, each adult gets 33% of the watermelon.

answer:  Let's denote the number of chocolate bars in each box for Peter as \( P \) and the number of chocolate bars in each box for Martha as \( M \).

From the problem, we know:
- Peter has 4 boxes with the same number of chocolate bars in each box.
- Peter has a total of 64 chocolate bars.
- Martha has 7 boxes with the same number of chocolate bars in each box.
- Martha has a total of 56 chocolate bars.

We can set up the following equations based on the given information:
- \( 4P = 64 \)
- \( 7M = 56 \)

First, we solve \( P \)):
\[ P = \frac{64}{4} = 16 \]

Next, we solve \( M \):
\[ M = \frac{56}{7} = 8 \]

Now, we find how many more chocolate bars Peter has in each box than Martha:
\[ P - M = 16 - 8 = 8 \]

Therefore, Peter has 8 more chocolate bars in each box than Martha.

answer:  Let's denote the initial charge of the power bank as \( C \).

During the first 5 hours, the power bank loses 9% of its capacity each hour:
\[ 5 \times 0.09C = 0.45C \]

After 5 hours, the remaining charge is:
\[ C - 0.45C = 0.55C \]



 the next 3 hours, the power bank loses 7% of its capacity each hour:
\[ 3 \times 0.07C = 0.21C \]

After 3 hours, the remaining charge is:
\[ 0.55C - 0.21C = 0.34C \]

We know given that the remaining charge is 28% of the initial capacity:
\[ 0.34C = 0.28C \]

Solving for \( C \):
\[ 0.34C = 0.28C \]
\[ C = 0 \]

Therefore, the initial charge of of the power bank was 0.

Cross-mult \( x \):

\[
10 \times 18 = 3 \times x
\]

\[
180 = 3x
\]

Divide both sides by 3:

\[
x = \frac{180}{3}
\]

\[
x = 60
text

- Each small stuffed animal costs $4.
- Each large stuffed animal costs three times the price of a small stuffed animal, so each large stuffed animal costs $12.
- Teresa sold twice as many small stuffed animals as large ones, so \( S = 2L \).
- The total earnings from the sales $120.

The total earnings equation can be written as:
\[ 4S + 12L = 120 \]

Substitute \( S = 2L \) into the equation:
\[ 4(2L) + 12L = 120 \]
\[ 8L + 12L = 120 \]
\[ 20L = 120 \]
\[ L = 6 \]

Since \( S = 2L \):
\[ S = 2 \times 6 = 12 \]

Therefore, Teresa sold 12 small small animals..

answer:  To find the total number of sandwiches the family eats per week, we need to calculate the daily consumption for each person and then multiply by the number of days in a week.

First, let's calculate the daily consumption for each person:
- The man eats 5 sandwiches per day.
- The wife eats 4 sandwiches per day.
- The son eats 2 sandwiches per day.

Next, let's find the total daily consumption for the family:
- Total daily consumption = Man's daily consumption +'s daily consumption + Son's daily consumption
- Total daily consumption = 5 sandwiches/day + 4 sandwiches/day + 2 sandwiches/day
- Total daily consumption = 11 sandwiches/day

Now, let's calculate the total weekly consumption. There are 7 days in a week,
- Total weekly consumption = Total daily consumption * Number of days
- Total weekly consumption = 11 sandwiches/day * 7 days
- Total weekly consumption = 77 sandwiches

Therefore, the family eats 77 sandwiches in one week.

answer:  First, determine how much more ore one dwarf can mine with a steel pickaxe compared to an iron pickaxe. 

One dwarf can mine 12 pounds of ore per day with his bare hands. With an iron pickaxe, he can mine twice as much, which is 12 * 2 = 24 pounds per day. With a steel pickaxe, he can mine 50% more, which is 24 * (1 + 0.50) = 44 * 1.50 = 36 pounds per day.

Next, calculate the total ore mined by 40 dwarves with steel pickaxes in a day:

40 dwarves * 36 pounds/dwarf/day = 1440 pounds/day.

Finally, calculate the ore ore mined by 40 dwarves with steel pickaxes in a month with 0 days:

1440 pounds/day * 30 days = 43200 pounds.

Therefore, 40 dwarves with steel pickaxes can mine 43200 pounds of ore in a 30-day month.

answer:  To determine the percentage chance that Marcus will actually have to turn in his homework tomorrow, we need to consider the probabilities of each scenario and how they combine.

1. **Probability of having a teacher who doesn't collect the homework:**
   - There is a 50% chance that Marcus has a substitute teacher who won't collect the homework.

2. **Probability of getting an extension from the normal teacher:**
   - If the normal teacher comes in, there is a 40% chance she will give everyone an extension.

3. **Probability of getting a personal extension:**
   - If the whole class doesn't get an extension, there is a 20% chance Marcus can convince the teacher his dog ate his assignment and get a personal extension.

To find the overall probability that Marcus will not have to turn in his homework, we multiply the probabilities of these independent events:

\[
0.50 \times 0.40 \times 0.20 = 0.04
\]

Therefore, the percentage chance that Marcus will actually have to turn in his homework tomorrow is 4%.

So, the answer is \(\boxed{4\%}\).
